## Document 031: 2026-10-30_［QE予測］7～9月期実質GDPは年率＋1.0％とプラス成長を予想（1次QE）_4f5741cf

7～9 月期実質GDP は年率＋1.0％と 
プラス成長を予想（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比＋0.2％（年率＋1.0％）と、4～6月期（前期比＋
0.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半期連続でのプラス成長を予想 
○ 個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資や民間在庫が押し上げる見込み。なお、公的在庫と輸入では
中東情勢を主因とする一時的な変動が生じ、成長率にプラス・マイナス両面で影響 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）はプラス成長 を
予想 
11月16日 （月） に、 内閣府から2026年7～9月期の 「四半期別GDP速 報（QE）」
（1次速報値）が公表される。現時点で入手可能な基礎統計の結果を踏まえ
ると、7～9月期の実質GDPは前期比＋0.2％（ 年 率＋1.0％） となる見込みだ。
4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半
期連続でプラス成長になると予想する（図表）。 
7～9月期は、内需の柱である個人消費が伸び悩む一方、 設備投資の拡大や、
製造業を中心とする在庫積み増しが成長率を押し上げるとみられる。その他
では、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要因として現れる
が、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイナス要因になるなど、 
図表 2026 年7～9 月期GDP（1 次速報）予測

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

### Page 2

2

中東情勢による一時的な変動も引き続き成長率に影響する見込みだ。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
個人消費は伸び悩み 。耐
久財の特需剥落や、 根強
い節約志向が影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％（4～6月期：同＋0.0％） と、2四半期連続で伸び
悩む結果になろう。消費の弱さの背景には、 複合的な要因があると考えられ
る。 
耐久財では、自動車とエアコンの「特需」剥落が押し下げ要因になろう。
自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に廃止されたこと
を受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていたが、8月以降は再
び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年4月の省エネ基準
引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要が生じていたが、
景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入った後は一服した
模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減少もエアコンなど
季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
食品やエネルギー、 各種消耗品などの非耐久財では、物価高を背景とする
根強い節約志向により、 弱含みが続いているようだ。今回のインフレ局面が
本格化する前の2020年と比較すると、非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6
年半でおよそ1.3倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上
昇が、 消費者の体感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、 購入数量の抑制
につながっている可能性がある。 
サービス消費は、 イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、 サービスや価格帯、 顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。 また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・ 宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、 レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投 資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
設備投資は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.9％） と、3四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスへ転じると予想する。 
直近の設備投資関連指標をみると、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財
総供給指数（除く輸送機械）が7～8月に大きく上昇した。また第3次産業活
動指数では、ソフトウェア業の実質売上高が同じく7～8月に増加したことが
確認でき、どちらも7～9月期の設備投資が拡大したことを示唆している。 
加えて、前期の4～6月期に発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特許権の海外
譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少） がなくなったこともプラス要因だ。た
だし、 これは裏を返せば、7～9月期のQEにおける設備投資の伸びが実態以上
に強い数字になるということでもあり、評価する際には留意が必要である。 
住宅投資は 前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.8％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.6％ ）と、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスとなろう。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～8月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上

### Page 3

3

昇による需要低迷に加えて、 足元では地震や台風などによる着工 ・ 進捗の遅
れが発生している可能性がある。 
民間 在庫は 2四半期連続
で成長率にプラス寄与 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、＋0.2％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラスが続く見込みだ。AI関連需要の強さなどを背景に、製
造業で在庫積み増しの動きが進んでいることが影響すると考えられる。な
お、4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を
押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足
が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響は一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要 は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラスに 
公的需要は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.8％）と、2四半期ぶりの前期
比プラスを予想する。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じるとみられることだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変
動の寄与度が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備
蓄の減少が小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公
的在庫の減少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.3％Pt程度のプラ
ス寄与になると見込まれる。 
その他の公的需要項目では、政府消費を前期比＋0.2％（4～6月期：同＋
1.7％）と予想する。4～6月期は高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化開始に
より高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした特殊要因が剥落し、緩やかな
増加ペースに戻ると考えられる。公共投資は前期比▲0.5％（4～6月期：同
▲0.5％）と、土木関連工事の減少を受けて2四半期連続のマイナスを見込む。 
輸出が小幅に減少する 一
方、 輸入が増加し、外需は
4四半期ぶりのマイナス
寄与 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.3％（4～6月期：同＋0.4％）と小幅に減少す
る一方、輸 入が同＋1.3％（4～6月 期：同▲1.7％）と増加に転じる見込みだ。
実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.3％Ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナ
スが予想される。 
輸入の増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化により4
～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホルム
ズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの輸入
量が確保されている。 
輸入はGDPの控除項目であることから、7～9月期は輸入増加が成長率の下
押し要因になる。 ただし、代替調達の進展に伴って国家備蓄原油の放出が抑
制され、 先述したように7～9月期は公的在庫変動が成長率にプラス寄与する
とみられることから、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果の一部は公的在庫変
動により相殺されるだろう。 
輸出では、前期の4～6月期に特許権の海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げ
た （これは4～6月期の設備投資の押し下げ要因でもある） 反動が現れ、 一時
的に下振れするだろう。一方、旺盛なAI関連需要が支えになり、半導体・電

### Page 4

4

子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸出は堅調に推移する見込みだ。インバウ
ンド需要 （非居住者家計の国内での直接購入） は、 日中対立の継続による中
国人訪日客の少なさに加え、 航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の伸び悩みに
より、低調な結果になると見込まれる。 
2026 年度後半 の実質 GDP
は小幅プラス と予想 。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期から減速するものの、4四半期
連続でプラス成長になると予想する。GDPの約半分を占める個人消費の伸び
悩みが足を引っ張っているが、設備投資や製造業を中心とする在庫積み増し
がけん引する格好だ。 
先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～2027年3月）の 実質GDP
は、 均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。個人消費が振るわな
い一方で、 設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろう。2026年度全体の
実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平均水準が押し上げら
れることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％程度とまずまずの
成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。 原油 ・ ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、 コア消費者物価指数 （生鮮食品を除く総合） は年度末にかけて前年比＋
3％程度まで上昇すると予想される（ただし、2026年末から政府の電気・ガ
ス料金支援策が実施されれば、0.4～0.5％Pt程度の押し下げ要因になる可能
性がある）。加えて、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを
中心とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、 一時的に消費が落ち込む可能
性がある。 
他方で、年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調
査） では、2026年度の設備投資計画 （ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投
資額 （除く土地投資額）） が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した （6月 調 査：
同＋8.8％）。生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、
設備の不足感が強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、 設備投資は今後拡大
傾向で推移していく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」 （特定生産性向上
設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、 日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、 企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、 また、 投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、 中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。

### Page 5

5

日銀は10月会合で 政策金
利を据え置き 。次回利上
げは12月と予想 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、 日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％） は、次回12
月17・18日の会合と予想する。 前回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて 「3カ月
に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、 日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の 「半年強
に1回」から加速していることを印象付けるものになろう。 
企業の価格転嫁行動は一段と積極化しており、植田総裁が9月会合後の記
者会見で 「政策の局面が変化した」と説明したように、日本銀行は「物価引
き上げ」 から 「物価上振れ抑制」 へと金融政策運営の舵を切っている。12月
の追加利上げ後も、 金利上昇の影響を確認しながら、 日銀は最終的に2027年
半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げていくだろう。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

## Document 077: 2026年11月9日投資研究週報

市場焦點
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉後，市場擔憂民主黨可能放緩AI資料中心的建設並提高對AI的監管，促使科技股
面臨估值修正壓力 。但從基本面觀察 ，2026年標普500企業獲利年增率預估已由 7月的26.5%
大幅上修至36.1%，其中資訊科技產業預估成長 64.5%，通訊服務亦有52.0%。大型科技企業
仍具備穩健現金流與持續投入AI基礎建設的能力，AI長期發展趨勢尚未改變，產業基本面不宜
因短期估值修正而過度看淡。
中國A股
• 短期來看A股反彈行情有機會延續 ，主因為美國期中選舉落幕 、中國政策環境偏寬鬆 ，以及A
股估值與情緒指標落在歷史相對低檔 ，具備超跌反彈條件，政策面方面，人行可能進一步降息，
政策利率由1.25%下調至1%，搭配IPO超募資金回購、穩定資金加大ETF買入力道，有助於改

善市場流動性並提振投資信心。
石油/黃金
• 波斯灣國家能源出口替代方案已略見成效 ，不過由於替代路徑運輸成本較高 ，能源價格仍居
高不下，觀察過去幾週出口及價格變化 ，推測在美伊關係未有明確變化的情境下 ，國際油價
將在每桶100美元附近震盪。
• 央行購金需求維持高檔 ，但相較去年同期累計未進一步放大 。在此情境下 ，短中期黃金價格
能否進一步突破的關鍵，可能取決於聯準會貨幣政策的調整空間。

• 選民不滿情緒發酵，民主黨在期中選舉取得重大突破：伊朗局勢久拖不決、通膨持續攀升，削弱選民對川普政府的支持，民
主黨因此在期中選舉中於參、眾兩院取得過半席次，新一屆國會將在2027年1月3日就職，進入白宮與國會分由不同政黨掌
控的「分裂政府」時代。州長選戰方面，民主黨也成功翻轉多個原由共和黨長期執政的州，州長席次占比睽違20年再度超越
共和黨，其中德州、喬治亞州、俄亥俄州及愛荷華州的選情翻轉尤其受到矚目。
• 國會制衡與州政府版圖變化，政策走向仍存不確定性： 儘管民主黨成功奪回眾議院，但在參議院尚未取得足以單獨推動重大
法案的關鍵席次，例如除了財政預算相關法案可以用預算調節程序簡單多數通過外，一般的法案都需要60票才能通過，因此
未來重大政策仍須仰賴跨黨協商，市場對政策劇烈轉向的疑慮可望有所緩解。不過，民主黨掌握更多州政府，也可能促使部

分州在川普剩餘兩年任期內，進一步制衡或抵制聯邦施政議程，並對2028年總統大選的選務布局產生重要影響。
美國民主黨贏得聯邦參議院、眾議院以及德克薩斯州州長選舉

• 美國經濟具韌性，紮根美股未來底氣：美國期中選舉後，市場擔憂民主黨可能放
緩AI資料中心的建設並提高對AI的監管，促使科技股面臨估值修正壓力。但從基
本面觀察，標普500企業獲利展望仍持續改善。2026年標普500企業獲利年增率
預估已由7月的26.5%大幅上修至36.1%，其中資訊科技產業預估成長64.5%，通
訊服務亦有52.0%。短期而言，市場對AI資本支出效益及投資回收期的疑慮，可
能使科技股評價面臨重新檢視，並進一步影響輝達、半導體、伺服器及相關供應
鏈；然而，大型科技企業仍具備穩健現金流與持續投入AI基礎建設的能力，AI長
期發展趨勢尚未改變，產業基本面不宜因短期估值修正而過度看淡。

美國政治藍色浪潮來襲：AI數據中心建設受阻與產業鏈資產重估
• 政治監管與地方風向轉變：AI基礎設施擴建面臨停工與審查的壓力，民主黨在多個關鍵州分贏得州長選舉，並伴隨地方公投
限制措施的升溫，使得原本蓬勃發展的AI數據中心建設計畫大幅放緩。
• 關鍵州分政策轉嚴：德州、喬治亞州與俄亥俄州等AI數據中心投資重鎮，當選民主黨州長普遍對數據中心開發採取強硬立場。
德州喊出暫停新建並取消稅收優惠，喬治亞州提出全州暫緩新項目，俄亥俄州與威斯康辛州亦對能源消耗、環境審查及稅收
補貼提出嚴格管制條件。
• 地方公投與審查週期拉長： 全美46項涉及數據中心的地方限制措施中 ，高達40項趨向收緊或已通過更嚴格限制 ，未來開發
項目將面臨更長審批週期、更嚴格電網接入審查及更高公用事業與監管合規成本。

• 未成熟開發商延期風險高：以全美儲備規模最大的德州為例，超過半數項目尚處於早期階段，且大量由缺乏營運經驗的初次
開發商主導。在監管與水電資源收緊下，此類項目極易面臨延期或取消的風險。
AI建設熱潮面臨民主黨藍色浪潮衝擊 46項地方數據中心措施投票結果分布
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.05

美國製造業景氣加速擴張，原物料成本壓力仍牽制通膨降溫
• 製造業擴張動能增強，生產與新訂單同步改善：美國10月ISM製造業PMI升至55.5，較9月54.5增加1%，連續第10個月
維持擴張；其中生產指數升至 59.0，創2021年11月以來新高，新訂單指數亦升至 55.5，顯示製造業接單及生產活動持
續增溫，且存貨指數回升至51.4，反映企業庫存由收縮轉為擴張，製造業景氣復甦動能進一步增強。
• 需求回升伴隨成本壓力，通膨降溫仍面臨挑戰：供應商交貨指數升至59.1，代表交貨速度持續放緩，連續11個月呈現相
同趨勢；客戶端庫存指數維持41.6，顯示庫存水位仍偏低，有利後續補庫存需求。然而，原物料價格指數雖由77.9降至
75.2，仍連續第25個月處於上漲區間，反映鋼鋁、石化及進口商品成本壓力尚未消退，可能使通膨降溫進程更加緩慢。

• 後市觀察：美國製造業景氣持續擴張，反映企業需求及經濟活動仍具韌性，有助支撐企業營收與獲利；惟原物料價格維
持高檔，可能增加通膨黏著性，使聯準會貨幣政策維持審慎，並對美債殖利率形成支撐。後續市場將關注製造業需求能
否延續，以及成本壓力是否逐步傳導至終端物價。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.11.3

資料來源：ISM Manufacturing PMI Report ， 2026.11.3

企業財務與行業分化：高槓桿新雲業者受創，巨頭發揮定價優勢
• 政策與審查趨嚴使得整體AI建設成本上揚，但在產業內卻呈現出極端的強者恆強、弱者面臨淘汰的兩極趨勢。
• 新雲公司(Neoclouds)面臨流動性與違約風險：若數據中心建設延誤或無法如期接入電網，收入推遲產生，而這類新興雲端
服務商普遍具高財務槓桿，高額利息費用持續累積，導致現金流期限錯配，將提高其再融資風險、擴大信用利差，甚至觸發
合約中的不可抗力條款與債務違約風險。
• 超大規模雲端巨頭(Hyperscalers)享受供應趨緊紅利：對科技巨頭而言，基礎設施擴建受阻反而是利多。已擁有現成數據中
心與電力保障的巨頭定價權大幅提升，算力供給緊俏能將雲端計算服務價格維持在高檔。此外，巨頭有能力自行籌資進行專
用發電或直接與公用事業公司談判，進一步拉開與新進入者的差距。

• AI六大支柱亮警報：支撐AI熱潮持續發展六大條件(訂單積壓、融資、算力需求、競爭、政治、聯準會)中，政治、競爭及聯
準會三項已現疲軟，特別是詞元(Token)成本持續下降、模型可替代性提高，低價模型加劇競爭，持續擠壓軟體獲利空間。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.05
詞元(T oken)成本持續下降• 小結與投資建議： 期中選舉引發的政策變革 ，正將AI
行業從無差別大舉擴建帶入嚴格監管與高成本的新階
段。供給側瓶頸(電網、土地、監管)將淘汰財務體質孱
弱、高度依賴外部融資的高槓桿新興開發商；供給受
限也鞏固已具備規模與資源保障的雲端巨頭壟斷優勢
與定價權。 投資建議避開高槓桿新雲與中小型開發商 ，
聚焦資本雄厚且自備水電資源的超大規模雲端巨頭 ，
密切關注聯準會貨幣政策與信用利差變化 ，AI行業的
金融條件正在收緊 ，若聯準會降息放慢或信用利差持
續擴大 ，AI供應鏈中的高估值軟體與硬體股可能面臨
新一輪估值修正。

AI資本支出持續攀升，現金流承壓加劇企業信用分化
• AI基礎建設投資擴大 ，企業融資需求同步升溫： AI競賽已由
模型及技術開發延伸至資料中心 、運算晶片與電力基礎設施
建置 ，帶動大型科技企業持續提高資本支出 。Microsoft、
Alphabet、Amazon等企業近期公布的財報均顯示 ，AI相關
投資已進入高強度建置階段 ，市場預估大型科技企業 2026年
AI基礎建設投資將超過7,000億美元，龐大資金需求也使市場
更加關注企業融資成本、資本配置效率及未來現金流表現。
• 財報反映投資成本，現金流與信用評等成市場檢驗重點： Q3
財報顯示，Alphabet及Amazon均因AI基礎建設投資大幅增
加，使自由現金流承受壓力；其中 Amazon近12個月自由現
金流轉為負值，Alphabet單季亦出現負自由現金流 。相較之

下，Microsoft仍維持正向自由現金流，反映企業財務承受能
力有所差異。此外，Meta因AI資本支出規模擴大及未來現金
流不確定性 ，遭標普全球調降信用評等展望至負向 ，顯示市
場已開始將AI投資效益納入企業信用風險評估。
• 市場看法： AI長期需求仍具成長潛力 ，但市場評價將逐步由
資本支出規模轉向投資報酬率 、自由現金流及財務槓桿管理 。
而具備穩定營運現金流 、較高資本回收效率及明確 AI商業化
成果的企業 ，較有能力維持投資動能；反之 ，若資本支出持
續攀升而營收及獲利未能同步跟上 ，將增加企業融資需求與
信用風險，並使科技企業間的評價差異進一步擴大。
大型科技企業擴大資本支出，承諾規模持續攀升
AI產業表外承諾大幅揚升
資料來源：Morgan Stanley Research，企業10-Q、10-K、8-K申報文件，ZeroHedge，
兆豐銀行彙整。

A股短期反彈行情可望延續，長期聚焦科技成長
• A股短期反彈行情可望延續 ，長期聚焦科技成長 ：從A股內部條件來看 ，
市場經歷長時間修正後 ，估值與情緒指標已降至歷史低檔 ，創業板 本
益比回落至約26.5%，全市場站上200日均線個股占比僅約一成 ，顯示
悲觀情緒已相當充分反映 ，搭配市場成交熱度回升 ，盤面觀察近期銀
行、消費等權值板塊 逐漸止穩，科技成長類股也開始出現籌碼與評價
修復，顯示市場輪動條件逐步成熟 。短期而言，A股反彈行情仍有機會
延續，主因為美國期中選舉落幕、中國政策環境偏寬鬆，以及A股估值
與情緒指標落在歷史相對低檔 ，具備超跌反彈條件 ，政策面方面 ，人
行可能進一步降息 ，政策利率由 1.25%下調至1%，搭配IPO超募資金
回購、穩定資金加大ETF買入力道，有助於改善市場流動性並提振投資

信心。
• 投資建議： 操作上可優先布局半導體 、AI硬體、通訊設備 、電子零組
件、醫藥及機器人等具成長題材的族群 ，並留意商業航太等新興應用 ，
不過，反彈過程仍須觀察政策落實程度 、企業獲利修復及海外市場變
化，中長期而言，A股仍以科技創新與科技服務實體經濟為核心主軸 ，
相關政策方向並未改變 ，建議維持科技成長為核心配置 ，搭配具景氣
復甦潛力的週期與消費產業，採取分批布局。

中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫
• 中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫：中國10月製造業PMI降至49.3%，低於市場預期，也跌破50榮枯線，
顯示製造業景氣再度轉弱 ，主要受到十一長假前需求提前釋放 ，以及前期海外聖誕訂單集中出貨後進入調整期影響 ，使需求與
生產動能同步放緩，新訂單指數和生產指數皆下降，顯示企業生產活動亦有所收斂 ，不過若與去年同期相比 ，新訂單與生產指
數分別增加0.6及0.1個百分點，顯示製造業基本面仍具一定韌性 ，景氣表現優於去年同期 ，此外企業對未來仍維持審慎樂觀態
度，生產經營活動預期指數為53.4%，雖較前月略降，但仍位於擴張區間。
• 展望後市：隨著專項債加速投放，及8,000億元政策性金融工具逐步轉化為實體投資，基建及製造業需求有望持續釋放，帶動裝

備製造、鋼鐵等產業鏈景氣改善，此外11月為企業衝刺全年營運目標的重要時點，訂單接單與生產活動將較10月回溫，產業表
現方面，科技業因受惠於政策扶持及產業升級趨勢，維持強勁動能，消費品製造業則有望受雙十一購物帶動成長。

民主黨主張監管政策 間接支撐黃金價格表現
• 期中選舉為黃金價格帶來短期支撐：民主黨主導國會加深兩黨政治僵局的擔憂，加上可能推動更嚴格的AI產業監管，市場擔
憂對美國經濟成長帶來負面影響，進而增加聯準會採取寬鬆貨幣政策的可能性，同時美元走貶也是上週支撐金價的因素之一。
• 央行需求基本面穩健：世界黃金協會最新報告指出 ，各國央行在9月持續增加黃金持有量，當月淨購買量達61噸，為今年迄
今最高的單月總量。同時數據顯示，全球各國央行所申報的黃金淨購買量為228噸，較去年同期下降 7%。
• 觀點：央行購金需求維持高檔，但相較去年同期累計未進一步放大，同時高水準的美債殖利率排擠黃金的避險需求。在此情
境下，短中期黃金價格能否進一步突破的關鍵，可能取決於聯準會貨幣政策的調整空間。雖然市場大致排除激進升息的預期，

但緩步升息仍是基本情境，預料近期黃金價格將在每盎司4,000~4,200震盪。消息面來看，美伊衝突維持僵局，不過若是荷
姆茲海峽議題能有重大突破，將為黃金帶來進一步上漲空間。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，資料統計至9月底，2026.11.4。

期中選舉牽動，上週國際油價大幅震盪
• 選舉結果令國際油價大跌：選舉結果出爐後，市場預期民主黨將在預算與軍事決策上牽制總統川普，促使中東僵局加速透過
外交協商落幕。結果公布當天布蘭特油價單日大跌約 9%，至每桶94美元，臨近週末則小幅反彈。上週油價整體收跌約16%，
主要反映選舉風險溢價消退，不過美伊僵局未變，油價仍在100美元附近震盪。
• 中東原油出口接近戰前水準，但成品油出口仍大幅落後：波斯灣國家正致力原油出口替代方案，目標擺脫荷姆茲海峽航運依
賴。石油出口量近幾週雖有所回落，但已接近戰前平均水準；然而，成品油出口動能恢復緩慢，目前仍遠不及戰前平均，這
也是造成全球能源通膨的主要原因。
• 觀點：波斯灣國家能源出口替代方案已略見成效，不過由於替代路徑運輸成本較高，能源價格仍居高不下，觀察過去幾週出

口及價格變化，推測在美伊關係未有明確變化的情境下，國際油價將在每桶100美元附近震盪。

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

2344 華邦電(Winbond)

報告日期： 2026/11/06  研 究 員：王蕎程 (Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要  
 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 為 8.06 元。 受惠記憶體
報價上漲與產品組合改善，第三季毛利率達 70.57% ，預估第
四季營收 839.78 億元， QoQ +2.0% ，毛利率 70.77% 。 
 擴產與 16 奈米轉進帶動 2027 年位元出貨成長。  高雄廠
Module A 新增產能及台中廠 Flash 擴產陸續貢獻，搭配
DDR4 、LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 需求，支持營運成長。  
 預估 2026 ／2027 年稅後 EPS 23.29 ／37.45 元。 AI 伺服器與
企業級 SSD 需求為主要動能， CUBE 及矽電容拓展新應用。

一、3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 營收持續季增 
華邦電為利基型記憶體廠，產品涵蓋客製化記憶體、NOR Flash 及
SLC NAND。3Q26 營收 823.31 億元，QoQ +37.6%、YoY +278.2%；
營業毛利 581.01 億元，毛利率 70.57%；營業利益 437.84 億元，營
業利益率 53.18%；稅後純益 354.22 億元， EPS 7.58 元。營收結構
以客製化記憶體占 58%、Flash 占 32%、邏輯 IC 占 9%。其中，客
製化記憶體平均售價季增近 60%，Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，為
第三季成長主因。展望第四季，公司表示 AI 需求維持強勁，部分
PC 與消費性客戶調整拉貨，整體營運仍將成長，惟增幅低於前幾
季。考量合約價格續升及產品組合改善，預估 4Q26 營收 839.78 億
元，QoQ +2.0%；毛利率 70.77%，營業利益 448.28 億元，稅後 EPS 
8.06 元。

二、高雄廠擴產，支應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 長期需求 
公司持續供應 DDR3、DDR4 及 LPDDR4，受惠主要記憶體原廠調
整產品配置，以及車用、工業客戶產品生命週期長、替換供應來
源不易，長期供貨需求具支撐。高雄廠 Module A 月產能預計由約
1.5 萬片提高至 2.4 萬至 2.5 萬片，新增產能於 2027 年陸續貢獻，
並提高 16 奈米製程比重，同時增加晶圓投入與單片晶圓位元產
出 。 法人看好擴產搭配製程轉進可帶動 2027 年 DRAM 位元出貨顯
著成長，中信預估增幅超過 100%。本報告採新增產能與製程效益
逐步反映的假設，預估 2027 年營收逐季成長。長期方面，董事會
通過 731 億元資本支出，用於高雄廠 Module B 土建、機電及長交
期設備，規劃月產能約 5 萬至 6 萬片，預計 2029 年下半年開始小
量出貨，並以長期供貨協議支應策略客戶需求。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：180 元 
      5 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：127.5 元 
     3.4 X 2027 EPS

三、AI 帶動 CUBE 與矽電容拓展新應用 
AI 伺服器機櫃內 GPU 、 BMC 、 SmartNIC 及交換器等元件均需 Flash
儲存韌體與設定資料，隨運算及管理架構複雜化，帶動使用顆數
與容量提升。公司預估供應 AI 生態系的 Flash 位元出貨量於 2026
年成長 112%，2027 年再增 67%，其中高容量 NOR Flash 為主要動
能；企業級 SSD 亦增加 DRAM 需求，擴大公司在伺服器供應鏈的
參與 。 法人看好台中廠擴產與高容量產品組合改善 。 新產品方面，
CUBE 主要布局 AI 加速器與推論應用 ， 預計 2027 年下半年增加出
貨；矽電容已小量出貨，既有產能多獲客戶預訂，並透過代工模
式切入 AI 晶片、先進封裝及高速光模組應用，逐步增加產品附加
價值與長期營收來源。  
四. 投資建議：買進，目標價 180 元  
華邦電為全球最大 NOR Flash 廠亦是利基型 DRAM 廠，AI 帶動各
式記憶體需求增加，產能仍供不應求下，獲利將逐季成長。公司
針對邊緣 AI 發展之 CUBE 解決方案將於 2027 年開始貢獻 ， 矽電容
亦為新的業務機會，中長期而言 AI、HPC、IoT、自駕車等新產品
新應用將帶動更多記憶體需求。考量到 1) AI 帶動之記憶體供不應
求將延續至 2028 年、2)華邦電今明年營收獲利創高、3)CUBE、矽
電容具成長潛力，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 180
元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
華邦電子於 1987 年九月由華新麗華集團轉投資成立，為自有品牌
IC 設計及製造廠商，並為全世界第五大 DRAM 供應商，且為國內
少數擁有晶圓廠之積體電路設計公司，同時也是全球利基型記憶
體的主要供應商。2008 年公司將邏輯 IC 事業分割成立新唐科技，
新唐產品線以消費性電子 IC 、電腦 IC 兩大產品線為主，並同時擁
有一座 6 吋晶圓廠。華邦電在 NOR Flash 為全球最大供應商，產
品上從中低容量市場拓展至中高容量市場。NOR Flash 主要同業為
旺宏、兆易創新、Infineon。

二.2026 年營運概況及展望

1. 2026 下半年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 2026 年 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 下半年，預估記憶體求維持強勁下，價格持續上漲，唯
因價格基期已高，加上傳統消費性產品追價力道有限，漲幅不若
上半年，不過預估今年隨著價格上漲，加上製程轉換帶動位元出

貨量增加，及新產能貢獻，營收逐季成長，毛利率持續提升，預
估華邦電 2026 年營收 2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%) ， 毛利率 67.17% ，
稅後淨利 1,047.92 億元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

3. 2027 年記憶體更加緊缺，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 帶動之記憶體缺貨狀況延續，加上全球新增產能
有限，緊缺情況更加嚴峻，而儘管終端電子產品需求不一，不過
未來 AI 落地後每單位產品之記憶體搭載量持續成長，使得記憶體
位元出貨提升 ，2027 年公司營運仍正向 。DDR4 在全球三大廠逐步
退出下價格自 2Q25 起漲，且因終端電子產品仍有一定需求，價格
將維持高檔，NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 亦因供給端增產有限，價格
逐步回升，其中 SLC Nand 因大廠退出，華邦將成為全球最大供應
商，而應用於 AI Server 的高容量 NOR Flash 持續供不應求，預期
將推升價格上行至 2027 年。華邦電製程技術方面，20 奈米 DRAM
產品仍為主要出貨主力，應用於 DDR3、DDR4、LPDDR 產品，16
奈米製程已開發完成，2027 年將有一定比例轉換至 16 奈米；NOR 
Flash 營收穩定成長 ， NAND Flash 24 奈米及 32 奈米 SLC Nand 今年
2Q 貢獻顯著提升。 因應記憶體需求強勁， 公司 Flash 與 DRAM 擴
產計畫進行中，華邦電 2025 年資本支出為台幣 55 億元，2026 年
將提升至 395 億元，其中生產設備資本支出約占 95%，大部分用
於高雄產能及製程提升，少部分用於台中廠製程提升，高雄廠產
能將由現在 15k 擴充至年底的 24k，屆時位元出貨量可望翻倍，同
時啟動高雄 Module B 投資計劃，規劃月產能可達 50k~ 60k，將分
階段擴充，預估 2027 年 1 月動工，2029 年 1 月裝機，2029 年底量
產。2027 年預估 DRAM 、NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 價格持續上漲帶
動營收獲利成長，預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛
利率 72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

4. CUBE 及矽電容為新成長動能 
AI 時代來臨，華邦電長期專注於利基型 DRAM ，針對 AI 將主攻邊
緣運算市場，邊緣運算之記憶體容量不需像 AI Server 那麼高，為
華邦電可著墨之區塊，因此華邦電提供 CUBE(Customized Ultra 
Bandwidth Element 客製化超高頻寬記憶體元件)解決方案，CUBE
具備超低功耗和超高性能、小尺寸、高成本效益，從而大幅提升
系統能力、回應時間和能源效率，以滿足邊緣運算平臺上快速增
長的 AI 應用需求。CUBE 優點在於 1)SoC 推疊在上面，散熱效果
較好、2) 可在 CUBE Die 上做穿孔(TSV)，降低成本、3) 3D 推疊
適合較小的終端裝置。目前華邦電與多家客戶開發 CUBE 方案，
預估 2026 年底開始貢獻，2027 年放量。另外因應 AI 晶片功耗劇
增、瞬間電流波動，以及先進封裝的空間限制，矽電容具有極低
的等效串聯電感與寄生電感，能在極短時間內補償電流，維持電

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 3Q26 毛利率升至 70.57%，EPS 7.58 元 
華邦電 3Q26 營收 823.31 億元(QoQ+37.6%、YoY+278.2%)，其中記
憶體事業營收 742.84 億元(QoQ+43.7%)。受惠客製化記憶體平均售
價季增近 60%、Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，合併毛利率提升至
70.57% ， 營業利益 437.84 億元 ， 歸屬母公司稅後淨利 354.22 億元，
EPS 7.58 元。展望第四季，記憶體合約價格持續上升，惟部分 PC
與消費性客戶調整拉貨，預估營收 839.78 億元(QoQ+2.0%)，毛利
率 70.77%，EPS 8.06 元。

二. 2026 年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 年下半年，AI 伺服器需求強勁，帶動 DDR4、LPDDR4
及高容量 Flash 價格持續上漲，惟第四季部分 PC 與消費性客戶調
整拉貨，營運成長幅度較第三季收斂。預估華邦電 2026 年營收
2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，稅後淨利 1,047.92 億
元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

三. 2027 年擴產與製程轉進帶動成長，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 伺服器與企業級 SSD 需求持續成長，DDR4、
LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 供需維持緊俏，加上高雄廠新增產能
與 16 奈米製程轉進，帶動位元出貨增加，CUBE 及矽電容亦逐步
貢獻營收。預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛利率
72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Wiwynn Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q beat on higher GM/OpM
Recent concerns revolve around potential data center delays,
though the supply chain has not seen any changes. However, we
lower our mid-term growth  rate to bake in some conservatism.
Our PT now implies a lower CY27 P/E at  12x. RR looks attractive
at 7.7x CY27 P/E vs. the 3Y  avg. of ~10x.
4Q revenue should continue to grow double-digits q/q: Wiwynn announced
October revenue at ~NT$165B (-4% m/m, +81% y/y). If we assume the October

monthly run-rate can be sustained, 4Q should grow at least 13-14% q/q. However,
we think ASIC server racks will continue to ramp-up through-out year-end, so we
forecast 4Q revenue to ~20% q/q to reach ~NT$520B (+78% y/y). Higher cost
structure and higher mix of ASIC server racks should drive margins to decline q/q to
~8.5% (-40bps q/q), resulting in net profit of ~NT$27.7B or EPS of NT$46.70.
ASIC/GPU projects remain on track: Our checks indicate that  Trainium3 server rack

production is ramping up well at Wiwynn, and with T3, Wiwynn is a more
comprehensive partner, also engaging in UBB and switch trays. It will also engage in
AMD's (covered by Joe Moore) MI455 Helios rack assembly for customers such as
Meta (covered by Brian Nowak) and Oracle (covered by Sanjit Singh). We forecast
Wiwynn to build ~8K Helios racks for CY27.
3Q results beat on better margins: Revenue was NT$434.6B (+63% y/y) with GM
of 8.9% (-40bps q/q), which is 90bps/30bps above MSe/Street. OP reached ~NT

$32.5B (+66% y/y) for OpM of 7.5% (+20bps q/q), which is 100bps/50bps above
MSe/Street, driven by operating leverage. Non-op losses were slightly higher than
MSe/Street mainly due to higher interest expense, resulting in net profit of ~NT
$23.4B (+52% y/y) for EPS of NT$42.09, 44%/10% above MSe/Street.
Reiterate OW with price target unchanged at NT$2,515 (12x/11x CY27/28e P/E) –
our higher EPS estimates are offset by a lower mid-term growth rate. Risk-reward is

highly attractive at 7.7x CY27e or 6.7x CY28e P/E vs. the three-year range of 6-16x,
with a strong pipeline of AI and non-AI server projects.

### Page 6

6
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We keep our price target unchanged at NT$2,515: This implies 12x CY27e P/E or 11x CY28
P/E, vs. its thee-year average forward P/E of 10.3x, which we think is fair, in view of
Wiwynn's AI server project wins and share gains for general-purpose servers in the next 1-
2 years.
We continue to use a residual income model to derive our price target. Key assumptions
include a cost of equity of 7.0% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 6% and risk-free rate of

1%), a medium-term growth rate of 4% (lowered from 6%), and a terminal growth rate of
3%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$3,210 and NT$1,470,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

below.

## Document 181: 261111_ms_asmedia

Source PDF: all report pdf/261111_ms_asmedia.pdf

Date: 2026-11-11

### Page 1

M
Update
ASMedia Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Inventory correction extends
into 1Q27
Inventory adjustment extends into 1Q27. Management sees improving
motherboard sell-through, but expects customer destocking to constrain shipments
in 4Q26–1Q27. AM4 exceeded 70% of chipset shipments in 3Q and should remain
dominant in 4Q, weighing on ASPs. Management indicated GM of around 50% in
4Q26–1Q27 as foundry and backend costs rise; alternative packaging/testing sources

should begin helping from 2Q27. October's stronger proprietary mix provided some
relief, but 2027 revenue guidance remains unfinalized, with 20% growth now
considered aggressive.
Recovery likely depends on execution from 2H27. ASMedia signed a new SOW
with the customer and currently sees itself as the sole supplier. Management hopes
shipments will start in 2Q27, with meaningful platform volume from 3Q27; ASPs
would be at least 50–60% above AM4. PCIe switches contributed 6–8% of revenue

after Gen4 yield and thermal issues delayed design-ins, versus a double-digit mix
target for 2027. USB4 is expected to more than double from 2026's depressed base,
although robotics remains small. New products can support recovery, but higher
development spending limits near-term operating leverage.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst

Valuation Methodology and Risks
ASMedia Technology Inc (5269.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8% (beta 1.3, risk-free
rate 2% and risk premium 6%), medium-term growth rate of 8.0%, payout ratio of 75% and
terminal growth rate of 5.2%.
Risks to Upside
n AMD gains market share thanks to better-than-expected performance.
n In-house designed products grow faster than expected.
n US-China tensions improve and US tariffs alleviate.

Risks to Downside
n AMD cannot get substrate supply, causing earnings downside for ASMedia.
n Intel adopts TSMC's technologies more aggressively to compete with AMD.
n Spec migration on USB 4 is slower.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.11.02  
잠시 쉬어갑니다

3Q26 Review 
기아는 3Q26P 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.4% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회했다. 영업이익 증
감 요인은 환율 효과(+5,290억원), 물량 효과(+4,420억원), 믹스 효과(-4,650억원), 
인센티브/가격 효과(-7,270억원), 재료비 영향(-3,680억원), 기타(+4,130억원)이다. 
판매 성장에도 높아진 수익성 부담 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.9% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.1만대
(+8.8%)를 기록했다. 미국 HEV와 서유럽 EV 판매가 각각 118%, 163% 증가하며 
글로벌 점유율은 4.0%까지 확대됐다. 다만, 판매 성장에도 원화 강세와 원자재 가격 
상승, EV 비중 확대에 따른 믹스 부담으로 수익성은 전분기 대비 하락했다. 유럽 내 
EV 판매가 늘어나면서 믹스가 악화되었으며, 전쟁으로 인한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 
재료비 부담은 전년 대비 약 3,700억 증가했다.  
연간 판매목표 335만대는 유지했으나, 4분기 수익성의 급격한 반등은 어려울 전망이
다. 향후 미국 텔루라이드·스포티지 HEV 공급 확대와 유럽 EV 판매 증가에 따른 고정
비 흡수가 수익성 회복의 핵심이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 155,000원으로 하향 
기아에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 실적 추정치 하향을 반영해 목표주가를 
155,000원으로 하향한다. 재료비 부담, 환율 효과 축소, 예상보다 큰 믹스 악화로 단
기 이익 눈높이 조정은 불가피하다. 다만 산업수요 둔화에도 주요 시장에서 점유율을 
확대되고 있다는 점은 긍정적이다. 현재 배당 수익률은 7% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높
은 배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

## Document 247: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4.pdf

 절대 1.9  2.4  (15.2)

 절대 (달러환산) 6.1  13.8  (8.6)

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

주: 1. EPS, BPS 및 PER, PBR은 지배주주 기준임 
 2. PER등 valuation 지표의 경우, 확정치는 연 평 균 주가 기준, 전망치는 현 재 주 가 기준임 
 3. ROE,ROA의 경 우, 자본,자산 항 목 은 연초,연말 평균을 기 준 일 로 함

## Document 259: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면  이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 285: Asia FX Outlook 2027_ Rotation underway but tech currencies should lead

Source PDF: all report pdf/Asia FX Outlook 2027_ Rotation underway but tech currencies should lead.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The recent sell-oﬀ in oil prices has provided some much-
needed relief to South Asian currencies. Looking into 2027, and
assuming the dollar does start to soften, we prefer to back the
tech-linked currencies of North Asia. These currencies are
backed by large current account surpluses – surpluses which
could be coming home

Deepali Bhargava, Lynn Song and Chris Turner
Executive summary
Asian currencies have had a mixed run in 2026. AI export-linked
currencies (the Korean won, Chinese yuan, Singapore dollar, and
Malaysian ringgit) generally outperformed, while less direct
beneﬁciaries such as the Indian rupee, Indonesian rupiah, and
Philippine peso each weakened by more than 5%. Deteriorating
political dynamics and supply-side shocks from higher energy
prices further weighed on growth prospects and added pressure on
these currencies. One notable exception among the tech-linked
economies was the Taiwanese dollar, which depreciated by around
5% despite strong current account balances, as a tech-sector sell-

### Page 2

oﬀ in recent months triggered equity outﬂows. In contrast, the
Singapore dollar delivered another year of strong gains, supported
by AI-related exports and stronger-than-expected economic
growth.
Post-midterms, there has been a rotation from North Asian to
South Asian currencies, driven by the tech sell-oﬀ and lower oil
prices, which have helped improve valuations in parts of South Asia.
Looking ahead, a softer dollar should provide a tailwind for Asian

FX in 2027. However, we see further downside risks for the PHP and
IDR given persistent external vulnerabilities and weaker capital
inﬂows, while tech-linked currencies such as the KRW and CNY are
likely to outperform as current account surpluses likely play a
larger role in driving performance. CNY appreciation should
continue, but with greater two-way volatility.
Still-strong current account support for tech currencies: We
remain bullish on the KRW and TWD. In our view, the AI cycle

may be plateauing, but activity remains at a high level. Strong
AI-related exports should continue to support current account
balances, while exporter FX conversion should help oﬀset
capital outﬂows. In addition, new measures to encourage the
repatriation of overseas assets could provide further support
for both currencies.
Rate diﬀerentials and resident outﬂows are becoming less
supportive for EM Asia: Most Asian central banks are further
ahead in their tightening cycles than the Fed. We expect most

to complete their hiking cycles by the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 and
begin preparing for policy easing thereafter. As a result, interest
rate support for Asian currencies is likely to diminish and
negatively impact rate-sensitive currencies like IDR the most. At
the same time, households and corporates continue to favour
foreign currency assets, reﬂecting still-elevated global yields
and a desire to diversify savings away from domestic
currencies. This preference for foreign assets could oﬀset some

of the support from strong current account balances and limit
the upside for Asian FX, even as the broader dollar outlook
becomes more favourable.

### Page 3

Oil and food price risks should largely be behind us by the
second half of 2027. This should beneﬁt oil-importing
currencies such as the PHP, IDR and INR. However, tighter global
ﬁnancial conditions mean capital inﬂows are unlikely to be
distributed evenly. Political uncertainty and the MSCI
downgrade will continue to weigh on investor sentiment
towards IDR, while weaker FDI inﬂows remain a challenge for
PHP. We remain more constructive on the INR, where growth

should stay relatively more resilient, supported by supply chain
diversiﬁcation, continued FDI inﬂows, and lower vulnerability to
swings in the AI cycle.
Currency sections
USD/CNY: Steady appreciation trend to face
two-way risks ahead
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
USD/CNY 6.67 Mildly Bearish 6.62 6.60 6.55 6.60
PBoC has overseen a smooth period of continuous moderate CNY
appreciation: USD/CNY has been on a steady downward trend over the

past year and has been one of the surprising outperformers amid a
strong dollar backdrop. We currently pencil in one 10bp rate cut from
the People’s Bank of China (PBoC) next year, along with further targeted
easing measures. Eventually, the interest rate spread should attract
some CNY bears into action. The PBoC’s stance could shift after being
supportive of further strengthening over the past year. We see some
signs of pushback against appreciation already; in an October policy

statement, the PBoC warned against one-sided and pro-cyclical
behaviour in the FX market, and in November, it hiked the foreign-
exchange deposit reserve requirement from 4% to 6%. If the PBoC
pushes back more strongly, it could well mark the end of the CNY
appreciation cycle.
CNY headwinds should remain on the radar: US-China yield spreads
widened to the highest levels in decades in 2026, and short-term
spreads could widen further as the Fed has started rate hikes, while the

PBoC is still easing policy. Growth is expected to be more stable in 2027
after a year of moderation in 2026. This year was also an unusually
weak year for outbound investment, as we saw the introduction of

### Page 4

tighter rules on outward direct investment, leading to a surprise
contraction of outward direct investment (ODI) after years of solid
growth. Similarly, a crackdown on overseas investment income may
have discouraged further outﬂows. If outbound investment normalises
in 2027, this could be a catalyst for a USD/CNY rebound.
Base case is for continued appreciation, but the path may not be so
smooth next year: Considering the foreign currency left overseas in

recent years, and with incoming foreign currency receivables with
China’s trade surplus set to hit a new record high in 2026, the stage is
set for further CNY appreciation. At this point, this view is widely
accepted, which in the near-term helps reinforce the trend, but also
raises the question of whether most market participants are already
fully positioned in this direction. While the CNY will remain a relatively
low-volatility currency, we don’t expect the straight-line appreciation of

the past year to continue for another year. We set an initial ﬂuctuation
band forecast of 6.40-6.80 for 2027, with a 2027 year-end forecast of
6.50. Two-way ﬂuctuation looks likely to return, and a strong CNY
appreciation bias in markets raises the risks for a stronger reversal.
USD/IDR: Renewed weakening pressures likely
for the IDR
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q2
USD/IDR 18232.00 Neutral 18000.00 18100.00 1820
Growth continued to surprise on the upside through the ﬁrst three

quarters of 2026, with GDP expanding well above expectations. Both
domestic and external demand contributed to the strong performance.
Exports remained supported by robust demand for manufactured
goods, while domestically, investment activity and household
consumption held up despite rising political uncertainty and persistent
capital outﬂows. Government spending accelerated and capital
expenditure remained strong, reﬂecting conﬁdence in Indonesia's

medium-term growth prospects. Fiscal deﬁcit targets were raised but
remain below the legislative cap of 3%. Importantly, stronger growth
has not come at the expense of price stability, with inﬂation remaining
well contained, supported in part by government subsidies. However,
this macroeconomic resilience has yet to translate into higher investor
conﬁdence and capital inﬂows, which is keeping IDR under pressure. As
a result, we expect Bank Indonesia (BI) to hike by 25bp in December,

with the last hike this cycle being delivered in 1Q27.

### Page 5

Political uncertainty and MSCI downgrade: Indonesian assets have
come under signiﬁcant pressure this year, with the rupiah among Asia's
weakest currencies and the Jakarta Composite Index (JCI) down more
than 42% year-to-date, making it one of the region's worst-performing
equity markets. Investor sentiment has been weighed down by rising
policy uncertainty under the Prabowo administration, concerns over
energy subsidies, and questions around the implementation of

structural reforms. These concerns have intensiﬁed following MSCI's
review of Indonesia's market classiﬁcation, with a potential downgrade
from Emerging Market to Standalone Market status. The decision is
expected by mid-2027, raising the risk of sustained portfolio outﬂows.
MSCI's concerns centre on market accessibility issues, including opaque
shareholding structures, suspected coordinated trading activity, and
insuﬃcient evidence of regulatory enforcement. If the downgrade is

announced, it could trigger billions of US dollars in foreign outﬂows from
Indonesia markets.
Indonesia's external position has deteriorated, with the current
account deﬁcit widening to $3.5bn in 2Q26 from near balance at the
end of 2025, and pressures are likely to intensify. Slowing export growth
has reduced support from the trade balance, while FDI inﬂows
weakened further in 1H26. As a result, Indonesia recorded a balance of
payments deﬁcit of $10bn in 1H26, the largest on record, highlighting

growing strains in the country's external accounts. At the same time,
foreign investors remain cautious amid concerns over ﬁscal slippage, as
higher oil prices and El Niño-related subsidy spending weigh on the
ﬁscal outlook. We therefore continue to see downside risks to the
rupiah. Narrowing rate diﬀerentials as BI nears the end of its rate hikes
and widening external deﬁcit inﬂows leave the currency increasingly
vulnerable. With FX reserves no longer rising meaningfully, Bank

Indonesia's ability to smooth sustained depreciation pressures may
become more constrained over time.
USD/INR: Downside risks for INR likely to be
diminished
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
USD/INR 94.04 Neutral 94.00 94.00 93.50 93.00
The RBI delivered its ﬁrst rate hike in four years, raised its growth and
inﬂation forecasts, and signalled a path of gradual policy normalisation.
While the tone was hawkish, the message was one of calibrated

tightening rather than an aggressive hiking cycle. Rate cuts are ﬁrmly

### Page 6

oﬀ the table, but the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) also appears reluctant
to tighten liquidity sharply or front-load rate hikes. Barring a material
inﬂation surprise from persistently high oil prices, a stronger-than-
expected El Niño impact on food prices, or a sharper INR depreciation,
we expect the RBI to continue tightening gradually. Our base case is for
a further 50bp of hikes over the next six months, taking the repo rate to
6.0%. With inﬂation projected at 5.2% in FY27, this would lift the real

repo rate to around 50bp, returning policy to positive real rate territory
but still below the RBI's longer-term comfort level of around 1%,
suggesting scope for further normalisation should inﬂation risks
materialise.
India remains in a relative sweet spot: Growth has remained resilient
through 2026, supported by robust domestic demand and a stronger
external backdrop. Export growth has held up well as ﬁrms continue to
diversify export destinations, while easing tariﬀ-related tensions with

the US and the new trade agreement with the EU have supported a
reorientation towards European markets, where export momentum has
strengthened. As a result, manufacturing activity has remained robust
whereas investment growth has been underpinned by strong
government spending. While India beneﬁted less from the AI-driven
export boom than North Asian economies, it is also less exposed to a
potential correction as the cycle matures. Structural reforms, ongoing

infrastructure investment, and resilient services exports should leave
India well positioned to outperform many regional peers as the AI cycle
normalises.
Downside risks have diminished: India’s external position has
weakened, with the current account deﬁcit projected to widen to 2.5%
of GDP in 2026 from 0.7% in 2025. While RBI measures to attract foreign
inﬂows helped support the balance of payments, frequent policy
adjustments have raised some concerns about policy consistency. The

more signiﬁcant headwinds, however, have come from higher oil prices
and India’s relatively limited participation in the AI-driven export boom.
Looking ahead, lower oil-related risk premia should improve India’s
terms of trade and reduce pressure on the INR, while the decline in oil
prices has encouraged a recovery in foreign portfolio inﬂows.
Nevertheless, reserve adequacy has declined recently following foreign
outﬂows from equity markets and recent FX intervention, and the RBI is

likely to use periods of INR strength to rebuild reserves. So, while
downside risks to INR have diminished, the scope for sustained
appreciation remains limited, leaving the currency broadly stable rather
than materially stronger.

USD/KRW 1333.00 Mildly Bearish 1325.00 1325.00 13
Domestic demand picks up, BOK stays hawkish: Korea seemingly has
been one of the biggest beneﬁciaries of the AI boom this year. Having
expected the Korean economy to grow by 1.8% at the start of the year,
the Bank of Korea (BOK) now expects GDP growth in excess of 3.3% this
year and above 2.9% in 2027. Encouragingly, strong exports and
investment are broadening into consumption at a time of cyclically low

unemployment (2.6%). In response, the Bank has hiked the policy rate
twice to 3.00% and is considering further tightening. Money markets
price a move close to 3.50%, which was the peak of the cycle back in
2023, when inﬂation was running above 6%. That looks like the
direction of travel for the policy rate, though the central bank remains
worried about surging house prices and high levels of household debt.
A U-turn in sentiment: Having traded as high as 1,550 in June, the

subsequent drop in USD/KRW to 1,350 has been eye-watering. Driving
that has been a turnaround in portfolio ﬂows. In May and June,
foreigners sold around $30bn of Korean assets each month. Those
outﬂows have since slowed. Clearly, the performance of Samsung and
SK Hynix, which together comprise around 50% of the Kospi index, is
going to have a big say here. But our house view of the AI story
plateauing at a high level suggests it is too early to call the Kospi a big

negative for the won. In any case, the correlation between the won and
the Kospi regularly ﬂips from positive to negative and is hard to rely on.
Instead, we think government measures to encourage greater
repatriation of FX export proceeds have been a bigger driver of won
gains recently.
Stronger growth can see more repatriation: We expect Korea’s 15-20%
current account surplus to play a more meaningful role in driving won
gains in 2027. Korea’s current account surplus is close to $50bn per

month and the challenge for Korean authorities is to get exporters to
bring that money home. Overseas reinvested earnings did see a big
drop in response to government measures in 2023. Since then, the story
has been mixed. But in 2027, we think a combination of higher
domestic growth rates, higher interest rates and new incentives (tax
breaks, etc.) can see more export earnings chase opportunities in
Korea. Looking at Korea’s real, trade-weighted exchange rate, we see it

around 5% below its 10-year average. A return to average levels would

Inﬂation in the Philippines remains the highest in the region, averaging
5.5% year-on-year in January to October 2026, with the latest reading
accelerating to 7.8% YoY, driven largely by higher fuel prices and their
pass-through into core inﬂation. The BSP's 50bp jumbo hike in October

was likely delivered in anticipation of this inﬂation surge. Looking
ahead, easing oil prices, a weaker USD and uncertainty surrounding
developments in Iran following the US midterm elections suggest that
Bangko Sentral ng Pilipinas (BSP) may prefer to assess the cumulative
impact of past tightening rather than move aggressively again. If oil
prices continue to decline and 3Q GDP growth disappoints, the case for
another jumbo hike appears limited. Nevertheless, we maintain our

base case for a ﬁnal 25bp rate hike in December, taking the policy rate
to 5.75%. While the inﬂation outlook is improving, core inﬂation
remains sticky, and the BSP is likely to retain a cautious tightening bias
to keep inﬂation expectations anchored and stay ahead of potential
upside risks.
Growth is likely to strengthen and recover to 5.0% in 2027, following a
weaker-than-expected ﬁrst half of 2026 that was weighed down by
ﬁscal underperformance. As government spending normalises,

favourable base eﬀects kick in, and lower energy prices support
household purchasing power, both business and consumer activity
should improve. Labour market conditions have softened, with
unemployment rising alongside weaker demand in government capex.
Wage growth in construction and services has lagged inﬂation,
constraining household incomes. The bright spot remains the
manufacturing sector, where wages have held up better, rising nearly
8.5% YoY in the ﬁrst half of the year. Labour demand in manufacturing

also remains robust, with job postings up almost 30% YoY, suggesting
continued strength in export-oriented and industrial sectors. Overall,
the growth outlook is poised to improve, although risks remain tilted to
the downside due to ongoing political uncertainty and potential
spillovers from Middle East tensions.

FX fundamentals remain challenging, pointing to continued currency
weakness: Narrow rate diﬀerentials and a widening current account
deﬁcit continue to weigh on the peso, while the BSP has drawn on its
foreign exchange reserves to smooth currency volatility. As a result,
reserve adequacy has deteriorated, with import cover declining to
around 6.2 months, the lowest level since 2018. While the weaker peso
has provided a boost to electronics exports and improved export

competitiveness, this has not been suﬃcient to prevent a signiﬁcant
widening in the current account deﬁcit. Strong import demand,
particularly for capital and intermediate goods, has more than oﬀset
export gains. Services exports and remittance inﬂows remain resilient,
but foreign direct investment inﬂows have weakened considerably,
reducing a key source of stable external ﬁnancing. Taken together, the
balance of payments outlook remains soft, suggesting limited scope for

sustained currency appreciation despite an improving growth backdrop.

Still-vigilant MAS: While the inﬂation outlook has improved following
the recent correction in oil prices, risks have not disappeared. Domestic

continue to see the balance of inﬂation risks as modestly skewed to the
upside in the near term, even though inﬂation is likely to begin
moderating from mid-2027 after these risks subside. The Monetary
Authority of Singapore (MAS) is likely to remain vigilant, closely
monitoring developments in energy prices, food inﬂation and domestic

demand conditions. Any renewed rise in domestic inﬂation pressures
could prompt a further tightening move in the January policy review.

in the ﬁrst three quarters, far exceeding expectations and highlighting
the economy's resilience. The economy has beneﬁted
disproportionately from the global AI investment cycle, which has
boosted demand for semiconductors, electronics and other high-value

manufacturing exports. At the same time, domestic demand has
remained ﬁrm, supported by a tight labour market, healthy wage

expectations and a softer US dollar should keep the Singapore dollar

## Document 323: COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Is COP still worth following? We think so. What matters for
businesses is whether this year’s COP31 can improve the
business case for action. Here are four areas to watch

Coco Zhang and Gerben Hieminga
The global climate landscape has changed signiﬁcantly since world
leaders committed 11 years ago to limiting global warming to well
below 2°C and pursuing eﬀorts to limit warming to 1.5°C.
Geopolitical tensions, policy shifts, trade protectionism, rising
energy demand… they have all made it harder for governments to
turn climate commitments into action.
That raises an important question for businesses: Is the Conference
of the Parties (COP) still worth following?
Our answer is yes.
Businesses do not just invest in climate targets; they invest in viable
projects and operations. But many of them still face economic and
practical barriers. Grid constraints and high electricity costs can
slow electriﬁcation. Greener industrial products can struggle to
Your privacy is important

our Cookie policy.

### Page 2

attract buyers willing to pay a premium. And climate adaptation
plans do not always translate into investable projects.
Businesses cannot solve these challenges alone. In a 2025 UN
survey of more than 2,000 CEOs across 128 countries, 92% said
strong global governance and coordinated policy are “critical” or
“important” to implementing their sustainability agendas.
That is why COP is still relevant. It can help align policy priorities,
improving investment conditions, and connecting businesses with

the right resources. At COP31, four areas will be particularly
important for businesses to watch.
Electriﬁcation marries economic growth
with sustainability
Electriﬁcation is increasingly important for economic growth,
energy security, and sustainability. Demand from AI, EVs, heat
pumps and industrial decarbonisation is accelerating electricity
use, which is growing twice as fast as overall energy demand.
Electricity also punches above its weight economically, accounting

for 23% of global ﬁnal energy consumption but powering 46% of
global GDP. This implies that high value-added sectors rely more on
electricity than the overall economy.
Electricity’s economic contribution
outweighs its share of energy use

### Page 3

Source: ING Research, International Energy Agency
COP31’s push to accelerate electriﬁcation reﬂects this trend. The
COP31 presidency has set a goal of increasing electricity’s share of
ﬁnal energy consumption from 23% today to 35% by 2035.
Getting there will require addressing a major constraint: the grid. As
we have written, grid congestion could slow electriﬁcation, increase
costs, and delay electricity-dependent economic activity.
The key question at COP31 is how governments turn this ambition

into action. We think eﬀective action could include:
Establish a detailed electriﬁcation roadmap with interim
benchmarks to guide progress and sustain momentum across
future COPs
Enhance knowledge-sharing platforms. The International
Smart Grid Action Network has extensive experience sharing
best practices, but could go further by translating knowledge
into direct technical assistance and ready-to-deploy models
Set country- or sector-level targets and pathways to encourage

industry alignment and commitment
Improve access to ﬁnance by expanding dedicated ﬁnancing
for developing countries, while lowering upfront costs in Europe
Improve the economics. Coordinate energy pricing reforms to
make electriﬁcation more competitive with fossil fuel
combustion alternatives, including by reducing taxes and grid
fees that raise electricity prices
Strengthen infrastructure by encouraging governments to
reduce permitting barriers (e.g. Europe and the US), advance

### Page 4

regulatory support for power demand ﬂexibility, and expand
cross-border grids where needed (e.g. Southeast Asia)
If COP31 delivers meaningful action in these areas, the implications
for businesses could be signiﬁcant. Stronger policy could accelerate
near-term grid connections and enhance long-term investment
conﬁdence. Meanwhile, companies may need to work more closely
with utilities and regulators to secure the power they need.
Green industrialisation remains critical

But electriﬁcation is only part of the story. Industry is another
major source of process emissions, with sectors such as steel,
cement, and aviation accounting for roughly 20% of the global
total. Cutting these emissions is particularly diﬃcult because many
industrial processes still have few cost-eﬀective alternatives without
lengthy site overhauls.
COP31 has therefore put green industrialisation high on the
agenda, building on COP30’s progress. Following the Belém

Declaration on Global Green Industrialisation at COP30, a coalition
led by the Brazilian and UK governments mobilised hundreds of
millions of dollars in concessional ﬁnance for Brazilian industries
and awarded funding to 12 hydrogen projects. Over time, these
eﬀorts could reshape manufacturing processes and investment.
But many industrial decarbonisation technologies still face
signiﬁcant barriers to scaling. Their high cost premiums mean
economics depend heavily on government support. Demand for

cleaner industrial products remains limited. And the necessary
infrastructure for many is still underdeveloped.
Hard-to-abate sectors’ green premium is
way higher than what companies are willing
to pay

Source: World Economic Forum, ING Research
COP31 is not setting a new level of ambition for green
industrialisation. It’s focusing on implementing the existing
agenda. One priority is narrowing the demand gap. COP31 can
coordinate governments to create more demand for low-carbon
industrial products through long-term public procurement. If such
eﬀorts gain momentum at COP31, they could provide the long-run
revenue certainty investors need to back green industrial projects at

scale.
Climate ﬁnance faces multiple gaps
To realise the targets and priorities set forth at COP, investment is
crucial. And total ﬂows are growing. Global climate ﬁnance reached
around $2tn in 2024 and continued to grow in 2025, although at a
slower rate.

But the headline number masks signiﬁcant gaps. First, even though
climate mitigation (i.e. emissions reduction) ﬁnance accounted for
$1.9tn of the $2tn total, the level falls well short of estimated
needs, which range from $7.6tn-$9.6tn through 2050. In areas such
as electriﬁcation and green industrialisation, funding is needed not
only for technologies, but also for the infrastructure and demand

needed to scale them.
Second, only $64bn of the total went exclusively to adaptation, an
area requiring surging investment because of intensifying climate
risks. Third, a substantial share of climate ﬁnance stays within
advanced economies or comes from domestic sources.
International ﬂows to help developing countries totalled only
$175bn, far below COP29’s target of $1.3tn per year by 2035.

Source: ING Research, Climate Policy Initiative, UN, UNEP, Natural
Resources Defense Council
More climate products and solutions are emerging in the latter two
segments. But their higher risks or weaker business cases can make
it diﬃcult to attract capital without supportive policies. Investors are
actively turning to blended ﬁnance, which uses public capital to
help de-risk projects and mobilise private investment. Yet many still
lack the data, resources and connections needed to move projects

forward. This is where COP31 can help. As we discuss in the next
section, it can help governments create more favourable
investment conditions and connect suppliers, buyers and investors.
Adaptation is becoming a business
imperative and opportunity
Business awareness of climate adaptation has grown, as many
businesses are experiencing more disruptions ﬁrsthand. Our
previous analysis also shows how drought on Europe’s Rhine River,
hurricanes in the US, and other extreme weather events are already

aﬀecting supply chains across sectors. In aggregate, physical
climate risks could cost companies in the S&P Global 1200 index
$1.2tn annually by 2050.
One barrier to corporate investment in climate adaptation is that it
is still seen primarily as a way to avoid losses. But adaptation can
also create business value. Investments in resilient assets, cooling
systems, supplier diversiﬁcation and climate-resilient materials can
improve reliability, competitiveness and long-term growth.

Research from the World Resources Institute suggests that every $1
invested in adaptation can generate up to $10 in beneﬁts. That
return could be even higher for highly climate-exposed sectors. For
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 8

example, the World Economic Forum estimates returns of $19–$43
per dollar invested in the food and beverage sector.
COP can help turn adaptation priorities into investable projects.
Since the Paris Agreement, countries have developed National
Adaptation Plans (NAPs), but many remain high-level and lack
speciﬁc, actionable projects. Recent COP eﬀorts have sought to
change this by encouraging governments to translate their NAPs
into portfolios of investable adaptation projects.

But identifying projects does not guarantee ﬁnancing. This is where
COP31 could make a diﬀerence. Its proposed Climate
Implementation Bridge (BRIDGE) initiative could make projects
easier for investors to identify and assess by centralising
information on technical requirements, risks, costs and beneﬁts.
Expanding the initiative to more countries could give more banks,
investors and companies access to potential adaptation
investments.
For businesses, a successful COP31 could therefore help unlock

capital for adaptation projects. The beneﬁts could extend
throughout supply chains, as companies increasingly depend on
the climate resilience of their suppliers to maintain reliable
operations.
Conclusion
The most eﬀective corporate sustainability strategies support long-
term growth, but they are more eﬀective with international
coordination and strong policy support. COP can bring
governments and businesses together to provide such a
framework, even amid geopolitical tensions and concerns over

slowing progress. COP31 may not deliver a landmark global
agreement, but practical advances in these four priorities could still
have immediate relevance for businesses.

## Document 351: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect 2027 to be a low-volatility year in FX, with central
banks and rate diﬀerentials ﬁrmly in the driving seat. We see a
dollar decline resuming from 2Q27, while diverging monetary,
growth and external dynamics create opportunities across
regions

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Deepali
Bhargava
Winners and losers in FX next year
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Not a breakout year for volatility
Our most important FX call for 2027 is the market environment. Our
macro-driven call is that lower oil prices and only a modest
recalibration in global monetary policy mean that FX volatility can
stay low. This is not 2022/2023, when major central banks
belatedly delivered 500bp tightening cycles to deal with double-
digit inﬂation. If 2026 has shown us anything, it is that FX markets
can prove quite resilient in this high-growth, high-inﬂation global

economy. And markets have proved quite resilient to geopolitical
risk as well.
For the majority of the last ﬁve years, the market has
overestimated FX volatility. There may be some structural factors at
play here, such as huge growth in ﬁnancial assets bringing in more
liquidity to the FX market and the greater use of FX pricing algos on
the buy-side, limiting the market fallout when trading conditions
thin.
Yet market volatility has not vanished. There are plenty of risks out

there, which we highlight in a dedicated section below. The same
applies for market liquidity. If the shock is big enough – such as
Liberation Day tariﬀs in 2025 or the start of the Iran war this year –
market depth can fall away quickly. The prospect of fat tails, low-
probability, high-impact event risks, is certainly a threat to global
FX markets. But the bar for these risks to raise volatility is a high
one.

### Page 3

Volatility has mostly undershot expectations
in 2026
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Look no further than central banks in
G10
In a year dominated by geopolitical risk and energy shock, currency
investors quickly moved beyond headline trading and increasingly
looked to short-term rate diﬀerentials as the primary guide for
currencies. We expect that to remain the case next year.
Central bank tightening is moving beyond its “insurance” phase,
requiring evidence that inﬂationary pressures are becoming

persistent. Oil prices will remain part of the story, but we think the
more important distinction is between economies where the shock
can generate more lasting inﬂation and those where it cannot. On
top of that, the central banks’ FX-speciﬁc approach will be key.
Remember how, in 2022-2024, the fear of ampliﬁed inﬂation
eﬀects from a weak currency prompted tighter ﬁscal policies
among some often dovish-leaning central banks.
Here are our top picks among G10 currencies for next year:

1. JPY – the best risk/reward. We don’t have a huge
outperformance of the yen in our forecasts, especially relative
to the euro. But that’s primarily due to our assumption that the
euro will drop its French-related risk premium by end-2028, and
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

a carry-favourable environment can somewhat contain yen
gains. However, we think the yen oﬀers the best risk-reward
proﬁle in G10. Alongside being sharply undervalued based on
long-term fundamentals, it faces limited risks of dovish
repricing as the Bank of Japan’s tightening cycle predated the
Iran war. It incidentally has strong safe-haven value against
some fat-tail risks (AI bust, ﬁscal dominance, dollar
debasement, some geopolitical events) and stands to be a

beneﬁciary of an ultimate capitulation in USD rates. Downside
risks are, crucially, limited by a coordinated Tokyo-Washington
eﬀort to prevent sharp USD/JPY rallies.
2. CHF – SNB turning pro-franc. If 2027 is the year when global
interest rates peak and then reverse, the opportunity cost of
holding the franc starts to fall. We also think the Swiss National
Bank will prove more tolerant of franc strength. Not only are
there fewer complaints about the strong franc from local

corporates, but Switzerland’s inﬂation is much lower than
trading partners and the SNB might again opt for nominal
franc appreciation to keep the real exchange rate stable.
3. NZD – The high-beta gamble. Among higher-beta currencies,
we see good potential for the New Zealand dollar next year. The
Reserve Bank of New Zealand has delivered a few dovish
surprises, but we think it is underestimating the inﬂation risks
for 2027: we expect at least two hikes, also helped by a fairly

robust growth backdrop. NZD should be favoured against the
Australian dollar to play a decline in global rates, as the
Reserve Bank of Australia may already shift to cuts in the third
quarter of 2027.
Sterling, dollar and AUD face largest dovish
repricing risk

### Page 5

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Dollar decline is delayed, not cancelled
Our baseline for global inﬂation in 2027 remains benign, leaving
most of our major central bank calls more dovish than current
market pricing. We expect the Federal Reserve to deliver one ﬁnal
hike in December 2026, taking rates to a 4.25% peak, but we don’t
think the additional hikes priced by 3Q27 will materialise. Modest
second-round eﬀects should fail to sustain inﬂation once the 2Q27

base-eﬀect decline comes through. Importantly, and unlike in other
economies, we think benign inﬂation rather than weaker growth
will be the main catalyst for a dovish Fed turn. That means markets
may be reluctant to price a large Fed cutting cycle too early, but it’s
equally an environment where we envision a Fed cut in 4Q27.
Ultimately, we expect a nearly 100bp drop in the 2-year USD swap
rate by the end of 2027, with most of the move concentrated in the

year’s second half.
We expect the dollar to weaken alongside that repricing, with
greater conviction in the downside story later in the year. The Fed
should remain cautious about turning dovish before clearer
evidence of disinﬂation emerges, while elevated funding rates will
continue to make it expensive to sell or hedge dollars until some
easing is priced in. And because we also hold dovish views on other
central banks, we don’t expect narrower USD short-term rate

diﬀerentials to trigger a particularly large dollar downturn. We
target a roughly 5% decline in DXY to 97 by end-2027.

### Page 6

Front-end rates to drive DXY lower
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Bond premium on the euro needs bad
news fuel
Our colleagues in the macro and rates team remain cautious about
a recovery in French sentiment in the next couple of quarters. Weak
growth (0.5% in 2027) should continue to make ﬁscal arithmetic
complicated, and we forecast a deﬁcit of 5.5% next year –
assuming a budget is passed – above the 5.0% government target.
Election frontrunner Marine Le Pen has signalled large spending

cuts, although their credibility remains in question. She is, anyway,
a more market-friendly candidate than left-wing rival Jean-Luc
Mélenchon.
We think markets will be wary of jumping into a meaningful OAT
relief rally before the presidential election (18 April-2 May) and likely
parliamentary elections in the summer are out of the way. We are
not forecasting another big selloﬀ in French bonds but think 125-
150bp will be the norm for the OAT-Bund 10yr spread into 2Q27,

before some tightening towards 100bp in 2H27.
The euro has been embedding a French risk premium since early
October, meaning a further material deterioration in sentiment
would probably be needed to generate additional idiosyncratic
pressure. Historically, the link between euro-area sovereign spreads

and FX has been patchy, with stress episodes often requiring a
steady ﬂow of negative news to sustain the correlation.
We therefore expect French political risk to keep euro sentiment
somewhat subdued into the elections and a subsequent bond-
market relief rally to oﬀer some support, but not to displace central

other words, we really need to see a signiﬁcant deterioration in the

EUR/USD to sustain a move below 1.10 through 2027.
EUR already embeds French risk premium
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Central Europe: Same shocks, different
paths for local FX
The Central Europe region is entering a year of divergence. The
Czech Republic, Poland and Hungary face similar risks from energy
prices, geopolitics and volatile global markets, but increasingly
diﬀerent monetary, ﬁscal and growth dynamics mean CEE3 is
becoming less of a single FX trade.

In the Czech Republic, the Czech National Bank has already
delivered two hikes, taking rates to 4%, and remains the most
hawkish central bank in the region. If inﬂation proves more
persistent, further tightening remains possible. Positive nominal
and real rate diﬀerentials and high credibility should therefore keep
the koruna relatively defensive during periods of global uncertainty,

### Page 8

while any easing of external risks could put it back on an
appreciation path.
Poland is moving diﬀerently. The National Bank of Poland is still
waiting to deliver its ﬁrst hike, leaving the zloty with less monetary
support for now. Strong growth and sizeable EU inﬂows remain
important anchors, but narrower rate diﬀerentials, loose ﬁscal
policy and rising political and rating risks have weakened PLN’s
relative appeal. We therefore see the zloty as more vulnerable to

higher global yields, energy prices and geopolitical risks.
Hungary is heading in the opposite direction, with the National
Bank of Hungary having cut rates in recent months. We expect
another cut only later next year, provided global conditions allow it.
Attractive carry, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should support the forint, while progress towards euro adoption
provides another positive longer-term story. The central bank’s

focus on the exchange rate should meanwhile limit the pace of
easing and keep EUR/HUF biased towards the stronger end of its
range.

Asia: Rotation underway, tech currencies
should lead

### Page 9

A softer dollar should provide a broad tailwind for Asian FX,
particularly in the second half of 2027, but we expect performance
to remain highly diﬀerentiated. We favour the Korean won and
Taiwanese dollar, expect further renminbi appreciation – albeit with
greater two-way volatility – and see continued downside risks for
the Indonesian rupiah and Philippine peso. We are more
constructive on the Indian rupee.
Current account strength should continue to favour the tech

currencies. Although the AI cycle may be plateauing, activity
remains high and strong export revenues should support the KRW
and TWD. Exporter FX conversion and measures encouraging the
repatriation of overseas assets should provide additional support.
CNY should also beneﬁt from strong external balances, although
we expect a less one-directional appreciation trend.
Rate support will become less favourable. Most Asian central banks
are further advanced in their tightening cycles than the Fed and

should ﬁnish hiking by early 2027 before preparing for easing.
Narrowing rate support should particularly weigh on the rate-
sensitive IDR. Continued household and corporate demand for
foreign assets could also oﬀset some of the beneﬁts from strong
current accounts.
Lower oil and food prices should help importers in the second half
of 2027, but capital ﬂows are unlikely to recover evenly. Political
uncertainty and the MSCI downgrade should also remain

headwinds for the IDR, while weaker FDI inﬂows leave the PHP
vulnerable. By contrast, we favour the INR, where resilient growth,
supply-chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI should provide
support, while leaving India less exposed to swings in the AI cycle.
Asia: change in current account balance and
local currency (2Q26 vs 2Q25)

### Page 10

Source: ING, Macrobond
Latam: Banxico’s neglect of the peso
stands out
We’ve said that central bank policy is going to be more important
than ever in 2027. And exchange rates can be an important part of
the monetary toolkit – especially in small, open economies. In a
world where central bank tightening cycles are being priced more
hawkishly, Banxico’s unabashedly dovish stance stands out.
When cutting rates to 6.25% in November, Banxico noted that,

because of slack economic conditions, the pass-through of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations to consumer prices would be limited.
The IMF puts that economic ‘slack’ – or the negative output gap – at
0.4% of GDP in 2027. That makes Banxico far less concerned about
a weaker currency than most other central banks.
In addition, Banxico has now cut the peso’s policy rate protection to
just 225bp over the Fed. That is a record low and leaves the peso
vulnerable to any deterioration in the external environment. We see

the peso as a relative underperformer in 2027.
Further south, the Brazilian real has been causing a lot of pain to
investors recently. The fear is that President Luiz Inácio Lula da
Silva’s pre-election giveaways will come home to roost next year.
His choice of Finance Minister is going to be important, as will any
progress on a 2027 budget. Preventing a bigger sell-oﬀ in the real,
however, is the independent central bank running very high real

## Document 402: Q3 Australian dwelling finance_ Bigger than expected investor pull back (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Australian dwelling finance_ Bigger than expected investor pull back (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
FINANCE BULLETIN
Steep fall
11 November 2026
Key points
• The value of new dwelling finance
approvals excl. refinance dropped
15.5%qtr, the biggest quarterly decline
on records going back to 1991. Annual
growth swung to –18.1%yr. While the

decline was widely expected, it was
larger than the median forecast of -8.5%
and Westpac’s forecast of –10%.
• The quarterly drop was mostly driven
by lower volumes: total loan numbers
down 15%qtr (-21%yr). Smaller average
loan size contributed a further 0.5ppts
to the value decline in the quarter.
• Investors were the primary driver,
volumes in this segment down
over 30%qtr and the value of new
loans off by nearly a third.
• Borrowing by owner occupiers

significant but somewhat milder
declines, both the volume and value
of new loans down 5.3%qtr.
• More weakness looks likely for Q4.
Q3 new dwelling
finance:

New finance approvals by segment: value

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The value of new dwelling finance approvals fell steeply
in Q3, a 15%qtr drop to be down 25% year to date. The
quarterly fall was the largest since the series began in
1991 while the fall over the last nine months is on a par
with the 2022 downturn, which was the largest on record.

The outcome was significantly weaker than the consensus
forecast of an 8.5% drop. The downturn reflects the
combined impact of rising interest rates and major housing-
related tax policy changes announced at the May budget
which have hit investor loans for the purchase of existing
dwellings particularly hard.
The detail shows volumes were the main driver, the number
of new loans dropping 15%qtr with the average loan size
accounting for 0.5ppts of the quarterly decline in the total

value of new loans.
The segment split showed a 5.3%qtr fall in new owner
occupier loan approvals and a whopping 30% drop in new
investor loans. Within the latter, the number of investor
loans for the purchase of existing dwellings (which no longer
receive ‘negative gearing’ and capital gains tax concessions)
fell 35.6%qtr and are down by a half over the year to date. In
contrast, the number of investor loans for the construction or
purchase of newly built dwellings (which are still eligible for

concessions) declined 8.7%qtr and 8.5% year-to-date. This
segment now accounts for 27.6% of all investor loans, up from
17% late last year.
Owner-occupier loans reflected the wider influence of interest
rate and market uncertainty. The detail here shows a more
pronounced 8.6%qtr drop in first home buyer numbers, a more
interest rate sensitive segment. High construction costs also
look to be a factor with the number of owner occupier loans for
the construction and purchase of newly built dwellings down

11.9%qtr. Lending for land purchases and renovations also
declined 12.9%qtr in value terms.
Today’s results were weaker than expected with a bigger
pull-back in investor activity than we assessed when the tax
policy changes were announced back in May. Some of this may
be an overshoot with uncertainty about policy changes and
the wider price correction discouraging investors who might
otherwise have considered purchasing even without the tax
benefits. That said, conditions look far from conducive for even

a muted bounce near term. More weak reads look likely for the
final quarter of 2026.
Bigger than expected investor pull back

使用量增及新的適應症 ，將使美國市場呈現穩定或
改善。

重點摘要
• 民主黨贏得國會兩院 ，增加可
以協商 ACA增強型補貼部分恢
復、醫療補助刪減延後的 機會 ，
但對 藥物服務管理機構的監管
持續。
• 管理式醫療機構維持利潤優先
於規模 、重定價改善醫療成本 ，
雖4Q26醫療成本季節性續升 ，
但預期 2027年改善 ，維持兩位
數獲利成長。
9000億美元。現ACA進行增強型補貼的恢復提案還
在進行中 ，須總統立法才能恢復 ，可以是談判交換
條件的一部分 ，醫療補助的刪減是已立法 ，分階段
生效，無法被推翻 ，只能延後刪減 ，其中對工作的
要求將於年底生效早於新國會就任 ，12/11臨時撥
款法案談判是可以納入談判作為局部修正的時間點 。
若醫療補助刪減延後 ，將有利於醫院 ，若ACA補貼

部分恢復將有利於 ACA型管理式醫療業者的投保人
數止跌回升。兩黨均支持對藥物管理福利機構(PBM)
的監管，已有法案讓Part D藥價與PBM的報酬脫鉤，
回扣必須全數轉交給計畫方 ，2028年生效，現業者
多將Part D回扣與價差收入轉為固定費用應對 ，對
激進者業務分拆的主張則可能以聽證 、調查形式出
現而非馬上立法 。但在州層級，阿肯色州禁止PBM
擁有藥局的法律在州政府上訴中 ，CVS已決定1H28
分拆藥局成兩家獨立公司應對 。就財報部分，UNH
3Q26財報仍維持業者利潤優先於規模與核保紀律，
合約重定價與刪除不賺錢合約 ，運用AI改善營運效
率、提升服務品質 ，在藥物福利管理主動讓利降低
政策風險 。聯合醫療保險 (UHC)因保費重定價與醫
療成本管控抵銷投保人數萎縮 ，Optum Health退
出虧損市場 ，聚焦高回報價值導向醫療運營紀律 ，
使此兩業務部門利潤率提升優於預期 。商業保險醫
療成本趨勢依然高 ，但已趨於穩定 ，醫療優勢計畫
(MA)利潤率已接近 2%-4%目標區間上限 ，公司自

評2027/2028年接近 75%會員可望維持在 4星以上
方案。醫療補助仍虧損 ，將透過州費率調整將緩步
走出低谷。4Q26醫療成本趨勢雖因季節性因素走升，
為今年獲利低點 ，但明年多項利潤率改善可期 ，維
持兩位數獲利成長。
